## <sub>1</sub> Intr<sub>o</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n

Recursive self-improvement (RSI) describes a system that improves the process by which it improves, not onl<sub>y</sub> its behavior. The idea <sub>g</sub>oes back to I. J. Good’s s<sub>p</sub>eculation about machines that desi<sub>g</sub>n better machines (Good, 1966) and to self-referential learners such as the Gödel machine (Schmidhuber, 2003), and recent di<sub>g</sub>ital a<sub>g</sub>ents have be<sub>g</sub>un to a<sub>pp</sub>roach it (Chen et al., 2026d). STOP (Zelikman et al., 2024) a<sub>pp</sub>lies a <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>g</sub>ram that im<sub>p</sub>roves code to its own source, and the Darwin Gödel Machine (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026c) <sup>l</sup>ets improve<sup>d</sup> co<sup>d</sup>ing agents mo<sup>d</sup>i<sup>f</sup>y t<sup>h</sup>emse<sup>l</sup>ves; <sup>b</sup>ot<sup>h</sup> ju<sup>d</sup>ge eac<sup>h</sup> can<sup>d</sup>i<sup>d</sup>ate wit<sup>h</sup> executa<sup>bl</sup>e tests. Yet a <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>em can ge</sub>t b<sub>e</sub>tt<sub>er a</sub>t <sub>a</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>proce</sub>d<sub>ure can</sub> k<sub>eep pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>e</sub>tt<sub>er vers</sub>i<sub>ons o</sub>f it<sub>, w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t an<sub>y</sub>thin<sub>g</sub> recursive ha<sub>pp</sub>enin<sub>g</sub>. RSI re<sub>q</sub>uires two further links (Section 2.2): what the s<sub>y</sub>stem retains from earlier improvements must make its next revision better, and the improver, the component that revises the learnin<sub>g p</sub>rocedure<sub>,</sub> must in turn use what it has <sub>g</sub>ained to hel<sub>p p</sub>roduce its own next im<sub>p</sub>rovement.

Physical RSI asks the same question of systems that learn by acting in the physical world, such as robots. Di<sub>g</sub>ital RSI su<sub>pp</sub>lies the mechanisms (lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e models that write reward code or controllers, archives of earlier variants, and automated ex<sub>p</sub>eriment loo<sub>p</sub>s); <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical RSI tests how these mechanisms use <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical ex<sub>p</sub>erience and whether the<sub>y</sub> hold under <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical constraints (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 1). The <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical settin<sub>g</sub> chan<sub>g</sub>es the ex<sub>p</sub>erimental conditions in four wa<sub>y</sub>s (Table 1). Interactive imitation learnin<sub>g</sub>, as in DA<sub>gg</sub>er (Ross et al., 2011) and HG-DA<sub>gg</sub>er (Kell<sub>y</sub> et al., 2019), showed h<sub>ow an exper</sub>t <sub>can correc</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>earner</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>earner</sub> it<sub>se</sub>lf <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>es.</sub> Lif<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ong ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t l<sub>earn-</sub> in<sub>g</sub> (Thrun and Mitchell, 1995) asks how knowled<sub>g</sub>e <sub>g</sub>ained on earlier tasks can reduce the real-world ex<sub>p</sub>erimentation needed for later ones; model-based RL, from D<sub>y</sub>na (Sutton, 1990) and PILCO (Deisenroth and Rasmussen, 2011) to Dreamer (Hafner et al., 2020), asks how recorded transitions can su<sub>pp</sub>ort <sub>p</sub>ractice inside a learned model; and skill-based a<sub>pp</sub>roaches, such as o<sub>p</sub>tions (Sutton et al., 1999) and the shared RoboEarth knowled<sub>g</sub>e base (Waibel et al., 2011), ask how an extended behavior can be stored and called a<sub>g</sub>ain. ## 2.1 Learning to Act

A f<sub>ew</sub> <sub>groups</sub> <sub>o</sub>f id<sub>eas</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>enoug</sub>h t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>res</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>rev</sub>i<sub>ew.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub> th<sub>em</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> <sub>runn</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> exam<sub>p</sub>le of the robot that kee<sub>p</sub>s missin<sub>g</sub> a <sub>g</sub>ras<sub>p</sub>.

Policies. A policy is the controller that maps what the robot currently observes (camera images, joint an<sub>g</sub>les, force readin<sub>g</sub>s, and <sub>p</sub>ossibl<sub>y</sub> a lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e instruction) to its next motor command; for the <sub>g</sub>ras<sub>p</sub>in<sub>g</sub> ro<sup>b</sup>ot, it turns a view o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e o<sup>b</sup>ject into t<sup>h</sup>e next movement o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e arm an<sup>d</sup> gripper. In t<sup>h</sup>e systems reviewed here, policies are usually neural networks. Generalist policies are trained on large collections of robot data s<sub>p</sub>annin<sub>g</sub> man<sub>y</sub> tasks, as in RT-1 (Brohan et al., 2023b), and sometimes man<sub>y</sub> robot bodies, or embodiments, as in Octo (Octo Model Team et al., 2024). A vision-language model (VLM) reads images an<sup>d</sup> text an<sup>d</sup> outputs text; ro<sup>b</sup>ot systems o<sup>f</sup>ten use one to propose goa<sup>l</sup>s or to ju<sup>d</sup>ge w<sup>h</sup>et<sup>h</sup>er an attempt succeeded. A vision-language-action (VLA) model is a generalist policy built on a pretrained VLM, so that it reads ima<sub>g</sub>es and an instruction and out<sub>p</sub>uts actions; RT-2 (Brohan et al., 2023a), O<sub>p</sub>enVLA (Kim et al., 2024), and $\pi _ { 0 }$ (Black et al., 2025) are exam<sub>p</sub>les. Man<sub>y</sub> recent <sub>p</sub>olicies out<sub>p</sub>ut a short se<sub>q</sub>uence of future actions at once, called an action chunk.

Learning from demonstrations. The simplest way to obtain a policy is imitation learning from demonstrations, which are often recorded while a <sub>p</sub>erson teleo<sub>p</sub>erates the robot (Ar<sub>g</sub>all et al., 2009). In behavior cloning (BC), the policy is trained to reproduce the demonstrated action in each recorded situation. BC learns onl<sub>y</sub> from recorded sit<sub>u</sub>ations<sub>,</sub> so its small errors can com<sub>p</sub>o<sub>u</sub>nd: if the <sub>g</sub>ri<sub>pp</sub>er drifts sli<sub>g</sub>htl<sub>y</sub> o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e o<sup>b</sup>ject into a state t<sup>h</sup>at no <sup>d</sup>emonstration covers, t<sup>h</sup>e po<sup>l</sup>icy may <sup>h</sup>ave no goo<sup>d</sup> action to ta<sup>k</sup>e. Interactive methods such as DA<sub>gg</sub>er (Ross et al., 2011) address this b<sub>y</sub> askin<sub>g</sub> an ex<sub>p</sub>ert what to do in th<sub>e s</sub>it<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>earner</sub> it<sub>se</sub>lf <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>es.</sub> I<sub>n prac</sub>ti<sub>ce, a person o</sub>ft<sub>en</sub> t<sub>a</sub>k<sub>es over w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t <sub>errs,</sub> and these interventions supply corrective training data.

Learning from rewards. In reinforcement learning (RL) (Sutton and Barto, 2018; Kober et al., 2013), the robot instead tries actions and receives a reward that scores the outcome, for example 1 for a stable grasp <sub>an</sub>d 0 <sub>o</sub>th<sub>erw</sub>i<sub>se.</sub> O<sub>n a rea</sub>l <sub>ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t<sub>, rewar</sub>d<sub>s</sub> d<sub>o no</sub>t <sub>arr</sub>i<sub>ve au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y: a person, a</sub> h<sub>an</sub>d<sub>-wr</sub>itt<sub>en c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>, or</sub> a learned reward model, such as a success detector, must judge each attempt. We call a component that supplies such judgments for learning an evaluator. Because rewards are often sparse and delayed, many methods learn a value function, or critic, that predicts how much future reward to expect; some systems <sub>use</sub> th<sub>e wor</sub>d <sub>cr</sub>iti<sub>c more</sub> l<sub>oose</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or any componen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>or</sub> h<sub>an</sub>d<sub>-wr</sub>itt<sub>en,</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>scores</sub> b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>or.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> advantage of an action measures how much better it is than what the policy would typically do in the same situation. Of-policy methods can learn from experience collected by earlier versions of the policy or by other sources, which they keep in a replay bufer. On hardware, every trial costs time, supervision, wear, and a reset that returns the scene to a usable starting state, so the source of the reward and the <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f <sub>ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s</sub> b<sub>ecome cen</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l <sub>resources.</sub>

Im<sub>p</sub>rovin<sub>g</sub> an existin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub>. L<sub>a</sub>r<sub>ge</sub> <sub>p</sub>r<sub>e</sub>tr<sub>a</sub>in<sub>e</sub>d <sub>po</sub>li<sub>c</sub>i<sub>es</sub> <sub>a</sub>r<sub>e</sub> r<sub>a</sub>r<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y</sub> r<sub>e</sub>tr<sub>a</sub>in<sub>e</sub>d fr<sub>o</sub>m <sub>sc</sub>r<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>ey</sub> <sub>a</sub>r<sub>e</sub> fine-tuned, or post-trained, on new data. A small residual policy can add corrections on top of the actions o<sup>f</sup> a <sup>f</sup>rozen <sup>b</sup>ase po<sup>l</sup>icy, suc<sup>h</sup> as a s<sup>l</sup>ig<sup>h</sup>t c<sup>h</sup>ange to t<sup>h</sup>e ang<sup>l</sup>e at w<sup>h</sup>ic<sup>h</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e gripper approac<sup>h</sup>es t<sup>h</sup>e o<sup>b</sup>ject. Distillation trains one policy to imitate another, for example to fold a specialist’s behavior back into a <sub>g</sub>eneralist. When a <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> learns a se<sub>q</sub>uence of tasks<sub>,</sub> it can for<sub>g</sub>et earlier ones<sub>,</sub> a <sub>p</sub>roblem studied in continual learning (Parisi et al., 2019); replaying stored data from earlier tasks is a common protection.

Models of the world. A world model, or dynamics model, predicts what will happen after an action, for instance the next camera ima<sub>g</sub>e or the next object <sub>p</sub>ositions (Ha and Schmidhuber, 2018; Hafner et al., 2020). Th<sub>e grasp</sub>i<sub>ng ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t’<sub>s con</sub>t<sub>ac</sub>t <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>s one examp</sub>l<sub>e:</sub> it <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er a</sub> t<sub>ouc</sub>h <sub>w</sub>ill b<sub>ecome a s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>.</sub> A robot can use such a model to plan, by imagining the outcomes of candidate actions, or to generate imagined experience on which a policy is trained. A physics simulator is a hand-built model of this kind; a learned <sub>wor</sub>ld <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>s</sub> fitt<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o recor</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a an</sub>d <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e wrong</sub> i<sub>n ways</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>a po</sub>li<sub>cy may</sub> l<sub>earn</sub> t<sub>o exp</sub>l<sub>o</sub>it<sub>.</sub>

Im<sub>p</sub>rovements outside the wei<sub>g</sub>hts. N<sub>o</sub>t <sub>eve</sub>r<sub>y</sub> im<sub>p</sub>r<sub>ove</sub>m<sub>e</sub>nt i<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>o</sub>r<sub>e</sub>d in n<sub>e</sub>t<sub>wo</sub>rk <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>s.</sub> A <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>e</sub>m can keep memories of earlier episodes, maintain a library of callable skills (Sutton et al., 1999), or write programs that compose perception and control functions (Liang et al., 2023). Large language models are increasin<sub>g</sub>l<sub>y</sub> used to write such code or to revise reward functions (Ma et al., 2024a).

## 2.2 Defining Recursive Self-Improvement

Wh<sub>ere</sub> <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>ence</sub> l<sub>eaves</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ange.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> <sub>cons</sub>id<sub>er</sub> l<sub>oops</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>ence</sub> <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d chan<sub>g</sub>e that afects later attem<sub>p</sub>ts or learnin<sub>g</sub>. Human demonstration<sub>,</sub> correction<sub>,</sub> reset<sub>,</sub> or a<sub>pp</sub>roval can <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n par</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oop: au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>on concerns w</sub>h<sub>o ma</sub>k<sub>es eac</sub>h d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on, w</sub>h<sub>ereas</sub> l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng concerns</sub> whether ex<sub>p</sub>erience chan<sub>g</sub>es it (Table 17 in A<sub>pp</sub>endix A). A failed u<sub>p</sub>date can also be informative b<sub>y</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>connec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>rea</sub>k<sub>s.</sub>

Generic <sub>p</sub>retrainin<sub>g</sub> witho<sub>u</sub>t a s<sub>u</sub>bse<sub>qu</sub>ent ex<sub>p</sub>erience–<sub>up</sub>date–<sub>u</sub>se com<sub>p</sub>arison serves as back<sub>g</sub>ro<sub>u</sub>nd<sub>,</sub> as does ada<sub>p</sub>tation confined to a sin<sub>g</sub>le attem<sub>p</sub>t. Ra<sub>p</sub>id Motor Ada<sub>p</sub>tation (Kumar et al., 2021), for exam<sub>p</sub>le, infers c<sub>u</sub>rrent conditions from recent histor<sub>y</sub> <sub>w</sub>itho<sub>u</sub>t retainin<sub>g</sub> the ada<sub>p</sub>tation. Ze<sub>v</sub>a’s interaction memor<sub>y</sub> is a bo<sub>u</sub>ndar<sub>y</sub> case: it carries across retries within an e<sub>p</sub>isode b<sub>u</sub>t not across e<sub>p</sub>isodes or learnin<sub>g</sub> ro<sub>u</sub>nds. Simulation-onl<sub>y</sub> and formal studies su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> enablin<sub>g</sub> mechanisms<sub>,</sub> while meta-learnin<sub>g,</sub> AutoML<sub>,</sub> and di<sub>g</sub>ital a<sub>g</sub>ents su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y p</sub>recedents (Section 2.3); direct evidence about <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical RSI must come from <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical s<sub>y</sub>stems.

A retained chan<sub>g</sub>e can serve four uses (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 4). It can choose or execute actions, <sub>p</sub>redict outcomes or su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>ractice, su<sub>pp</sub>ort reuse (for exam<sub>p</sub>le, callin<sub>g</sub> a saved recover<sub>y</sub> or consultin<sub>g</sub> <sub>g</sub>uidance for a new program), or change how later data are collected, judged, and turned into updates. These uses are usually carried b<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>olicies, world models, external knowled<sub>g</sub>e, and learnin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>rocedures, res<sub>p</sub>ectivel<sub>y</sub> (Sections 3.1 and 3.2). They can overlap: a policy can collect training data, a model can judge outcomes, and a memory can <sub>g</sub>uide an u<sub>p</sub>dater. Each stud<sub>y</sub> is discussed mainl<sub>y</sub> under the use its ex<sub>p</sub>eriment tests<sub>,</sub> re<sub>g</sub>ardless of <sub>w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>au</sub>th<sub>ors</sub> <sub>ca</sub>ll it <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-evo</sub>l<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>recurs</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-</sub>i<sub>mprov</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub>

Learner, learning procedure, and improver. The learner acquires task capabilities; the learning procedure determines what experience to collect, how to judge it, and how to construct updates. An improver proposes or selects revisions of that procedure. It may be a person, a fixed rule, a search process, or a learned com<sub>p</sub>onent<sub>,</sub> with knowled<sub>g</sub>e in memor<sub>y,</sub> wei<sub>g</sub>hts<sub>,</sub> code<sub>,</sub> or <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts. From better behavior to recursion. We distin<sub>g</sub>uish four claims (Table 3), and evidence for an earlier claim does not establish a later one. Behavioral improvement means better execution under a common task test. A later-learning benefit means that retained data, weights, models, skills, or code help a subsequent learner. Procedure improvement means that a revised collector, evaluator, editor, or updater teaches better than its earlier version. This comparison requires matched learners, which difer only in the component under test and share startin<sub>g</sub> conditions, resources, and evaluation <sub>p</sub>rotocol (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 8).

We operationalize recursive self-improvement through two further links, which this review uses as empirical criteria for attributing a gain to recursion. How far a result supports recursion. For R1 and R2, we distinguish mechanism only, where machinery could change the improver; an uncontrolled result, consistent with a link but without a control that withholds only what was acquired; and a controlled result, which tests that contribution with matched learners and bud<sub>g</sub>ets (Section 4.4). The claim and its level of su<sub>pp</sub>ort are se<sub>p</sub>arate.

What lets gains carry forward. Four conditions recur across these claims. Informative feedback distinguishes the remaining learning need. Usability means that the receiving component can represent, optimize, and execute the change. Robustness to distribution shift asks whether the change stays useful as the policy, model, task, or hardware changes, including changes caused by improvement itself. Continued practice asks whether another informative trial remains safe and afordable. These four conditions overlap <sub>an</sub>d <sub>serve</sub> <sub>as</sub> di<sub>agnos</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> l<sub>enses</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> <sub>va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 4<sub>.</sub>2 <sub>exam</sub>i<sub>nes</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ures, an</sub>d Fi<sub>gure</sub> 3 <sub>connec</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>ese con</sub>diti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e uses an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ms a</sub>b<sub>ove.</sub>

Rein<sup>f</sup>orcement <sup>l</sup>earning. Stan<sup>d</sup>ar<sup>d</sup> RL improves a po<sup>l</sup>icy un<sup>d</sup>er an o<sup>b</sup>jective an<sup>d</sup> up<sup>d</sup>ate mec<sup>h</sup>anism chosen before trainin<sub>g</sub> (Sutton and Barto, 2018). Run re<sub>p</sub>eatedl<sub>y</sub> on hardware, as in HIL-SERL (Luo et al., 2025) and RECAP (Ph<sub>y</sub>sical Intelli<sub>g</sub>ence et al., 2026), it <sub>y</sub>ields behavioral im<sub>p</sub>rovement, and its data or <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> can s<sub>upp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> a later-learnin<sub>g</sub> benefit. RL loo<sub>p</sub>s can also be nested inside an o<sub>u</sub>ter o<sub>p</sub>timization over <sub>p</sub>art of the learnin<sub>g p</sub>rocedure. ## 2.5 Benchmarking Self-Improvement

Most com<sub>p</sub>arisons in this review run in simulation, and the benchmarks difer in what the<sub>y</sub> stress (Table 5). LIBERO (Liu et al., 2023a) su<sub>pp</sub>lies se<sub>q</sub>uential mani<sub>p</sub>ulation suites, and LIBERO-PRO (Zhou et al., 2025b) pertur<sup>b</sup>s manipu<sup>l</sup>ate<sup>d</sup> o<sup>b</sup>jects, initia<sup>l</sup> states, tas<sup>k</sup> instructions, an<sup>d</sup> environments to test w<sup>h</sup>et<sup>h</sup>er <sup>b</sup>e<sup>h</sup>avior de<sub>p</sub>ends on the ori<sub>g</sub>inal cues; LIBERO-Plus (Fei et al., 2025) varies seven factors, includin<sub>g</sub> camera view<sub>p</sub>oint, initial robot state, and sensor noise. RoboCasa (Nasirian<sub>y</sub> et al., 2024) broadens household task covera<sub>g</sub>e, and RoboTwin 2.0 (Chen et al., 2025c) and RoboDojo (Chen et al., 2026<sub>g</sub>) cover bimanual mani<sub>p</sub>ulation. VLABench (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025d) and Meta-World (Yu et al., 2019) su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> further mani<sub>p</sub>ulation suites, and SIMPLER (Li et al., 2024) mirrors real robot setu<sub>p</sub>s for <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> evaluation. Navi<sub>g</sub>ation studies use Habitat (Savva et al., 2019) and R2R-CE (Krantz et al., 2020), and household a<sub>g</sub>ents use EmbodiedBench (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025). Exce<sub>p</sub>t for LIBERO’s lifelon<sub>g p</sub>rotocol, which scores transfer and for<sub>g</sub>ettin<sub>g</sub> alon<sub>g</sub> a task se<sub>qu</sub>ence<sub>,</sub> these s<sub>u</sub>ites test a s<sub>y</sub>stem at one <sub>p</sub>oint in time<sub>,</sub> and a claim abo<sub>u</sub>t im<sub>p</sub>rovement across ro<sub>u</sub>nds still needs a <sub>p</sub>rotocol on to<sub>p</sub> that fixes what carries over between rounds and what sta<sub>y</sub>s held out (Section 4.2). A recent benchmark <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>e mo<sub>v</sub>es in this direction: it <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>v</sub>ides no in-domain demonstrations<sub>,</sub> carries ex<sub>p</sub>erience over between re<sub>q</sub>uests within a de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment<sub>,</sub> and names how <sub>q</sub>uickl<sub>y</sub> an a<sub>g</sub>ent becomes com<sub>p</sub>etent in a de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment as a <sub>q</sub>uantit<sub>y</sub> of interest (Cai et al., 2026). Ph<sub>y</sub>sRSI-eval (Li et al., 2026j) fixes what carries over between ro<sub>u</sub>nds<sub>,</sub> in sim<sub>u</sub>lation. RoboCoach-Bench (Lei et al., 2026) collects 32 <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s</sub> f<sub>or agen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>wr</sub>it<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>rev</sub>i<sub>se</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng co</sub>d<sub>e.</sub> Di<sub>g</sub>it<sub>a</sub>l b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s o</sub>f <sub>recurs</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> self-im<sub>p</sub>rovement isolate related <sub>q</sub>uestions. PAST-Bench (Xue et al., 2026a) turns retained ex<sub>p</sub>erience on and of across ordered task se<sub>q</sub>uences, and RSIBench-Data (Men<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026a) has a<sub>g</sub>ents revise trainin<sub>g</sub>-data strate<sub>g</sub>ies for a fixed model. RSIG<sub>y</sub>m (Men<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026b) measures the fraction of the remainin<sub>g p</sub>erformance <sub>g</sub>a<sub>p</sub> that a<sub>g</sub>ents close across benchmarks.

ConRFT (Chen et al., 2025d) reaches 96.3% avera<sub>g</sub>e success on ei<sub>g</sub>ht real tasks after 45–90 minutes of online fine-tunin<sub>g</sub>, and VLAC (Zhai et al., 2025) raises real success from about 30% to 90% within 200 e<sub>p</sub>isodes. Retention asks whether earlier competence survives later learning or deployment, and a later-learning benefit on a new task is forward transfer. Corrections take the second route. HIL-SERL (Luo et al., 2025) builds on SERL (Luo et al., 2024), <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>com</sub>bi<sub>nes o</sub>f<sub>-po</sub>li<sub>cy</sub> l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>rewar</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>ers, an</sub>d <sub>rese</sub>t <sub>po</sub>li<sub>c</sub>i<sub>es, an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>dd<sub>s on</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> h<sub>uman</sub> interventions so that corrective transitions inform the critic that <sub>g</sub>uides the actor. SiLRI (Zhao et al., 2025) treats human i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erven</sub>ti<sub>ons as poss</sub>ibl<sub>y su</sub>b<sub>op</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>l<sub>:</sub> it <sub>poses rea</sub>l<sub>-wor</sub>ld RL <sub>as a cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>bl<sub>em w</sub>h<sub>ose</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t each state is set b<sub>y</sub> the uncertaint<sub>y</sub> of the interventions<sub>,</sub> solved with a state-wise La<sub>g</sub>ran<sub>g</sub>e multi<sub>p</sub>lier<sub>,</sub> and r<sub>eac</sub>h<sub>es</sub> 90% <sub>success o</sub>n r<sub>ea</sub>l m<sub>a</sub>ni<sub>pu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s</sub> in <sub>a</sub>t m<sub>os</sub>t h<sub>a</sub>lf th<sub>e</sub> tim<sub>e</sub> HIL-SERL n<sub>ee</sub>d<sub>s.</sub> Th<sub>e sa</sub>m<sub>e</sub> r<sub>eco</sub>rd<sub>e</sub>d transition can become a value tar<sub>g</sub>et, a wei<sub>g</sub>hted action tar<sub>g</sub>et, or a conditionin<sub>g</sub> variable, as RAPolic<sub>y</sub> (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026a), SI-EFM, and RECAP illustrate, and which of these the learner receives chan<sub>g</sub>es what it learns.

BEE (Zhao et al., 2026b) uses the consistenc<sub>y</sub> of human corrections to constrain RL u<sub>p</sub>dates to a residual <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy aroun</sub>d <sub>a</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> VLA<sub>.</sub> O<sub>n</sub> th<sub>ree rea</sub>l t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>one s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>,</sub> it <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>es</sub> 91<sub>.</sub>2% <sub>average success</sub> a<sub>g</sub>ainst 57.5% for an RL baseline at a matched online-data bud<sub>g</sub>et. RL Token (Xu et al., 2026b) instead <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>s a</sub> VLA t<sub>o e</sub>x<sub>pose a co</sub>m<sub>pac</sub>t r<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>,</sub> k<sub>eeps</sub> it fr<sub>o</sub>z<sub>e</sub>n<sub>, a</sub>nd tr<sub>a</sub>in<sub>s a s</sub>m<sub>a</sub>ll <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>o</sub>r–<sub>c</sub>riti<sub>c o</sub>n th<sub>a</sub>t r<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t online. On four real tasks, minutes to a few hours of practice speed up the hardest phase by up to 3× and raise success as ju<sup>d</sup>ge<sup>d</sup> <sup>b</sup>y a <sup>h</sup>uman operator, <sup>f</sup>or examp<sup>l</sup>e <sup>f</sup>rom 20% to 65% on screw insta<sup>ll</sup>ation. Outsi<sup>d</sup>e mani<sub>p</sub>ulation, SELFI (Hirose et al., 2024) fine-tunes a <sub>p</sub>retrained navi<sub>g</sub>ation <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> with online model-free RL whose Q-values keep the model-based objective of its ofline pretraining, improving collision avoidance and<sub>,</sub> in a <sub>u</sub>ser st<sub>u</sub>d<sub>y,</sub> social com<sub>p</sub>liance in real en<sub>v</sub>ironments.

Reward-<sub>g</sub>uided <sub>p</sub>ost-trainin<sub>g</sub> of VLAs has scaled mainl<sub>y</sub> in simulation<sub>,</sub> while its most reliable results come from smaller hardware studies. VLA-RL (Lu et al., 2025) on LIBERO (Liu et al., 2023a) and � (Chen et al., 2025b) run in simulation, and FLaRe (Hu et al., 2025a) and Sim<sub>p</sub>leVLA-RL (Li et al., 2026c) add real-robot results. In ei<sub>g</sub>ht-task hardware studies, ConRFT (Chen et al., 2025d) reaches 96.3% avera<sub>g</sub>e success after 45–90 minutes of online fine-tunin<sub>g</sub> and RL-100 (Lei et al., 2025a) succeeds in 1,000 of 1,000 evaluation e<sub>p</sub>isodes. F<sub>rom</sub> <sub>proposa</sub>l<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>po</sub>li<sub>c</sub>i<sub>es.</sub> F<sub>ee</sub>db<sub>ac</sub>k th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>can</sub> <sub>ran</sub>k b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>or</sub> l<sub>eaves</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>secon</sub>d <sub>c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ce:</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> th<sub>e</sub> im<sub>p</sub>rovement sho<sub>u</sub>ld live. Choosin<sub>g</sub> amon<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osals can im<sub>p</sub>rove exec<sub>u</sub>tion witho<sub>u</sub>t retrainin<sub>g,</sub> as V-GPS (Nakamoto et al., 2024) does b<sub>y</sub> rerankin<sub>g</sub> a frozen <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub>’s <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osals with an ofline value function; PA-RL (Mark et al., 2024) also im<sub>p</sub>roves sam<sub>p</sub>led actions under a critic and trains the <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> on them with a supervised loss. Learned judges can steer a frozen policy in the same way. UF-OPS (Attarian et al., 2026) trains success and time-to-success verifiers once on the <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub>’s labeled rollouts and selects amon<sub>g</sub> sam<sub>p</sub>led action chunks at run time<sub>,</sub> with re<sub>p</sub>orted <sub>g</sub>ains of 25 to 80 <sub>p</sub>ercenta<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>oints on five real Aloha tasks. World-Coherent Decodin<sub>g</sub> (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026b) learns durin<sub>g</sub> de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment to rank a f<sub>rozen wor</sub>ld <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l’<sub>s samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>u</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e m</sub>i<sub>sma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d f<sub>rames.</sub> I<sub>n</sub>

RoboTwin 2.0 sim<sub>u</sub>lation<sub>, u</sub>nder the a<sub>u</sub>thors’ limited-randomization <sub>p</sub>rotocol<sub>,</sub> it raises Hard-s<sub>p</sub>lit s<sub>u</sub>ccess from 55.80% to 60.90%. Kee<sub>p</sub>in<sub>g</sub> scorin<sub>g,</sub> im<sub>p</sub>rovement<sub>,</sub> and <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> chan<sub>g</sub>e se<sub>p</sub>arate avoids the need to ada<sub>p</sub>t <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y g</sub>radients to each action re<sub>p</sub>resentation<sub>,</sub> whether iterativel<sub>y</sub> denoised ch<sub>u</sub>nks in Dif<sub>u</sub>sion Polic<sub>y</sub> (Chi et al., 2023), discretized tokens in O<sub>p</sub>enVLA (Kim et al., 2024), or flow matchin<sub>g</sub> in $\pi _ { 0 }$ (Black et al., 2025). Selection <sub>g</sub>ains can sta<sub>y</sub> in the selector or be absorbed into the <sub>g</sub>enerator. VERITAS (Zhan<sub>g</sub> and Shah, 2026) behavior-clones trajectories steered toward agreement with a visual path proposed by a VLM. Q-Planning (Giridhar et al., 2026) instead leaves the cloned policy unchanged and updates a Q-function f<sub>rom success</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> O<sub>n</sub> it<sub>s</sub> t<sub>wo rea</sub>l<sub>-ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s,</sub> t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>rewar</sub>d<sub>s come</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> h<sub>uman ep</sub>i<sub>so</sub>d<sub>e</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>success</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>measure</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>ep</sub>i<sub>so</sub>d<sub>es</sub> th<sub>emse</sub>l<sub>ves.</sub>

B<sub>o</sub>th <sub>rou</sub>t<sub>es s</sub>t<sub>ay</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r proposa</sub>l<sub>s:</sub> if th<sub>e genera</sub>t<sub>or never pro</sub>d<sub>uces a grasp</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>avo</sub>id<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> slip, no selector can recover one. A residual policy modifies the proposed motion while keeping the base <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> as a <sub>p</sub>rior. ResiP (Ankile et al., 2025) trains one in simulation and then distills it into a vision <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> co-trained with real demonstrations. DSRL (Wa<sub>g</sub>enmaker et al., 2025) instead steers the in<sub>p</sub>ut noise of a frozen difusion <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub>, searchin<sub>g</sub> onl<sub>y</sub> within its learned distribution. DPPO (Ren et al., 2025) fine-tunes the difusion <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> itself and transfers a simulation-trained assembl<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> to hardware. Ex<sub>p</sub>andin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osals hel<sub>p</sub>s onl<sub>y</sub> if the resultin<sub>g</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>erience teaches the <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> where it needs hel<sub>p</sub>, which PLD (Xiao et al., 2026a) tests directl<sub>y</sub>. ## 3<sub>.</sub>1<sub>.</sub>2 W<sub>or</sub>ld M<sub>o</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> f<sub>or</sub> P<sub>re</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d P<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>ce</sub>

Ph<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s are s</sub>l<sub>ow an</sub>d <sub>cos</sub>tl<sub>y, so a na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l <sub>way</sub> t<sub>o s</sub>t<sub>re</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h th<sub>em</sub> i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> l<sub>earn a mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e wor</sub>ld <sub>an</sub>d <sub>p</sub>ractice inside it. A world model <sub>p</sub>redicts how a <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical state ma<sub>y</sub> chan<sub>g</sub>e after an action<sub>;</sub> for a <sub>g</sub>ras<sub>p,</sub> it mig<sup>h</sup>t pre<sup>d</sup>ict w<sup>h</sup>et<sup>h</sup>er t<sup>h</sup>e o<sup>b</sup>ject wi<sup>ll</sup> stay <sup>b</sup>etween t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>fi</sup>ngers w<sup>h</sup>en t<sup>h</sup>e gripper c<sup>l</sup>oses. T<sup>h</sup>e pre<sup>d</sup>iction can serve as a plan that must be translated into motion, as imagined experience from which a policy is trained, or as predicted features supplied to a policy. Each role tests a diferent form of usability: success in <sub>one ro</sub>l<sub>e says</sub> littl<sub>e a</sub>b<sub>ou</sub>t th<sub>e o</sub>th<sub>ers.</sub> G<sub>enera</sub>ti<sub>ve v</sub>id<sub>eo mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s o</sub>f<sub>er a</sub>ll th<sub>ree</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>aces: a genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> serves as a <sub>p</sub>lan in UniPi (Du et al., 2023), as a controllable simulator in UniSim (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2024b) and, learned without action labels, in Genie (Bruce et al., 2024), and as <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub>-trainin<sub>g</sub> data in DreamGen (Jan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025). Cosmos (NVIDIA et al., 2025) develo<sub>p</sub>s world foundation models for <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical a<sub>pp</sub>lications, WISA (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025a) and PISA (Li et al., 2025a) tar<sub>g</sub>et <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical consistenc<sub>y</sub>, and SPIRAL (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026d) (unrelated to the SPIRAL <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> loo<sub>p</sub> of Section 3.1.1) u<sub>p</sub>dates its <sub>g</sub>enerator with a critic that <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>s</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>a</sub>dh<sub>erence</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>rea</sub>li<sub>sm.</sub> A <sub>wor</sub>ld <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>can</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> <sub>en</sub>t<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>proce</sub>d<sub>ure</sub> b<sub>y</sub> jud<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> candidate <sub>p</sub>olicies or chan<sub>g</sub>es before the<sub>y</sub> reach hardware (Sections 3.2.2 and 4.4).

U<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o p</sub>l<sub>an an</sub>d t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n.</sub> Cl<sub>ass</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s supp</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>e ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> i<sub>ngre</sub>di<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> D<sub>y</sub>na (Sutton, 1990) interleaves real and modeled ex<sub>p</sub>erience, PILCO (Deisenroth and Rasmussen, 2011) and PETS (Chua et al., 2018) account for model uncertaint<sub>y</sub>, MBPO (Janner et al., 2019) kee<sub>p</sub>s ima<sub>g</sub>ined rollouts short to limit com<sub>p</sub>oundin<sub>g</sub> error, self-su<sub>p</sub>ervised visual <sub>p</sub>lannin<sub>g</sub> (Ebert et al., 2017) <sub>p</sub>lans throu<sub>g</sub>h learned video <sub>p</sub>rediction, and latent world models (Ha and Schmidhuber, 2018; Hafner et al., 2020) train <sub>p</sub>olicies in com<sub>p</sub>act d<sub>y</sub>namics, as Da<sub>y</sub>Dreamer (Wu et al., 2023) did on <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical robots. A model’s value for control need not track its <sub>p</sub>rediction accurac<sub>y</sub> (Lambert et al., 2020; Farahmand et al., 2017; Grimm et al., 2020).

Plannin<sub>g</sub> first ex<sub>p</sub>oses an interface <sub>p</sub>roblem: a <sub>p</sub>redicted video of the <sub>g</sub>ri<sub>pp</sub>er closin<sub>g</sub> is not <sub>y</sub>et a robot command. Joint-embeddin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>redictive architectures <sub>p</sub>redict in learned feature s<sub>p</sub>aces and <sub>p</sub>lan toward <sub>g</sub>oals there, as V-JEPA 2- AC (Assran et al., 2025) and DINO-WM (Zhou et al., 2025a) do, and D-JEPA (Liu et al., 2026e) ali<sub>g</sub>ns com<sub>p</sub>etin<sub>g</sub> <sub>re</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>u</sub>t<sub>ures w</sub>ith th<sub>e can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>execu</sub>t<sub>es</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Ph <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>ro er</sub>ti<sub>es mus</sub>t <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n rea</sub>d<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e:</sub> i<sub>n</sub> PokeWorld (Tan et al., 2026a), <sub>p</sub>redictin<sub>g</sub> touch makes stifness decodable whereas merel<sub>y</sub> su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> touch as an in<sub>p</sub>ut does not, and PSG-JEPA (Yan et al., 2026) adds <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>rioce<sub>p</sub>tive objectives where forward <sub>p</sub>rediction alone omits such structure. DreamerV3 (Hafner et al., 2025), which reconstructs observations, and TD-MPC2 (Hansen et al., 2024), which does not, bracket the desi<sub>g</sub>n s<sub>p</sub>ace. Retrieved histor<sub>y</sub> in Mem-World (Zhen<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026b), force–tor<sub>q</sub>ue <sub>p</sub>rediction in MuSe (Clark et al., 2026), and <sub>p</sub>redicted fin<sub>g</sub>erti<sub>p</sub> wrenches in TORL-VLA (Zhen<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026a) su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> distinctions that camera data alone can miss.

WorldEcho (Chen et al., 2026f) shows that models trained onl<sub>y</sub> on ex<sub>p</sub>ert trajectories, <sub>g</sub>iven commands outside that distribution<sub>,</sub> either i<sub>g</sub>nore the commanded motion or <sub>p</sub>roduce visuall<sub>y</sub> invalid rollouts, and CausalWorld (Ahmed et al., 2021) lets <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sical and visual variables be chan<sub>g</sub>ed inde<sub>p</sub>endentl<sub>y</sub>. Across embodiments, <sub>p</sub>article-based action re<sub>p</sub>resentations (He et al., 2026c) ofer a shared <sub>g</sub>eometric interface, while XEWorld (Chen et al., 2026i) finds that current models bind joint commands <sub>more rea</sub>dil<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>am</sub>ili<sub>ar appearance</sub> th<sub>an</sub> t<sub>o</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>era</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> ki<sub>nema</sub>ti<sub>cs.</sub> A <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>ll<sub>y p</sub>l<sub>aus</sub>ibl<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>may s</sub>till l<sub>ac</sub>k d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on-re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e, a usa</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ace, or correc</sub>t <sub>responses</sub> t<sub>o</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erven</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

We use memory for stored information that must be interpreted when it is used, skill for a callable behavior with an execution interface, and runtime or software for the lo<sub>g</sub>ic that retrieves, com<sub>p</sub>oses, monitors, or re<sub>p</sub>airs those behaviors (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 7). The same artifact can cross these boundaries (a recover<sub>y</sub> written down as text becomes a skill once a controller can execute it), but the distinction identifies who uses the chan<sub>g</sub>e next; for all three, the <sub>q</sub>uestion is whether a chan<sub>g</sub>e kept outside model weights is usable. On the path to RSI, external knowledge is also where a system can kee<sub>p</sub> the re<sub>p</sub>air knowled<sub>g</sub>e that recursion re<sub>q</sub>uires (Section 2.2).

Earlier s<sub>y</sub>stems ke<sub>p</sub>t diferent kinds of artifacts. RoboEarth (Waibel et al., 2011) shares models and action reci<sub>p</sub>es amon<sub>g</sub> robots. Code as Policies (Lian<sub>g</sub> et al., 2023) writes <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>g</sub>rams a<sub>g</sub>ainst <sub>p</sub>erce<sub>p</sub>tion and control APIs, and Inner Monolo<sub>g</sub>ue (Huan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2023) feeds execution feedback back into <sub>p</sub>lannin<sub>g</sub>. Im<sub>p</sub>lementations come from demonstrations in LOTUS (Wan et al., 2024) and Uni-Skill (Xie et al., 2026b), and from a <sub>p</sub>erson when the librar<sub>y</sub> cannot carr<sub>y</sub> out the <sub>p</sub>lan in HaLP (Parakh et al., 2024). ## Synthesis

Improving a learner and improving its learning procedure are distinct

<sub>•</sub> P<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>ce s</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>ld b<sub>e se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>or w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t it t<sub>eac</sub>h<sub>es.</sub> A <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ure, a use</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l <sub>correc</sub>ti<sub>on, an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> l<sub>earna</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k dif<sub>er, an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>s</sub>hift<sub>s as</sub> th<sub>e po</sub>li<sub>cy</sub> i<sub>mproves.</sub>

• Judgment and updating can themselves change. Refreshed evaluators, retrieved guidance, and learned editors chan<sub>g</sub>e later trainin<sub>g;</sub> safet<sub>y,</sub> reset<sub>,</sub> and verification services kee<sub>p</sub> <sub>p</sub>ractice <sub>p</sub>ossible.

• The re<sub>v</sub>ised decision needs a do<sub>w</sub>nstream test. In each case the im<sub>p</sub>rover’s knowled<sub>g</sub>e chan<sub>g</sub>es; what remains to be shown is that this <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ange, ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an a</sub>dd<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s, compu</sub>t<sub>e, or</sub> h<sub>uman</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>, ma</sub>k<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e nex</sub>t <sub>proce</sub>d<sub>ure</sub> b<sub>e</sub>tt<sub>er.</sub>

Two kinds of reuse sit close to R1 without reachin<sub>g</sub> it. ASPIRE (Lu et al., 2026a) retrieves <sub>g</sub>uidance from earlier <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>g</sub>ram re<sub>p</sub>airs, and the code-transfer stud<sub>y</sub> (Kuroki and Tan<sub>g</sub>, 2026) su<sub>pp</sub>lies o<sub>p</sub>timized <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>g</sub>rams as references; both im<sub>p</sub>rove how a fixed codin<sub>g</sub> model constructs later task controllers (Section 3.1.3), which is evidence about constructin<sub>g</sub> behavior rather than about revisin<sub>g</sub> collection<sub>,</sub> su<sub>p</sub>ervision<sub>,</sub> or u<sub>p</sub>dates. LMPC (Lian<sub>g</sub> et al., 2024) trains the model that writes reward code for a model-<sub>p</sub>redictive controller on <sub>p</sub>ast t<sub>eac</sub>hi<sub>ng sess</sub>i<sub>ons, so</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t t<sub>eac</sub>hi<sub>ng nee</sub>d<sub>s</sub> f<sub>ewer</sub> t<sub>urns.</sub> I<sub>n s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on, a success</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l <sub>sess</sub>i<sub>on on unseen</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s</sub> takes 1.9 rather than 2.4 chat turns (Section 2.3). That reward code drives real-time control rather than an RL trainin<sub>g</sub> run, and the reci<sub>p</sub>e that trains the model sta<sub>y</sub>s fixed. Di<sub>g</sub>ital <sub>p</sub>recedents. Di<sub>g</sub>it<sub>a</sub>l <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>e</sub>m<sub>s a</sub>l<sub>so sepa</sub>r<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e app</sub>l<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g a</sub> fix<sub>e</sub>d <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> r<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>pe</sub> fr<sub>o</sub>m m<sub>a</sub>kin<sub>g</sub> th<sub>e</sub> u<sub>p</sub>dater itself an object of search. Ex<sub>p</sub>ert Iteration (Anthon<sub>y</sub> et al., 2017) alternates tree search with trainin<sub>g</sub> a network to imitate the search, then uses that network to <sub>g</sub>uide the next search; STaR (Zelikman et al., 2022) re<sub>p</sub>eatedl<sub>y g</sub>enerates rationales, kee<sub>p</sub>s those that lead to correct answers, and fine-tunes on them. Both re<sub>p</sub>eat a fixed loo<sub>p</sub>. Prom<sub>p</sub>tbreeder (Fernando et al., 2024) evolves both task <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts and the mutation <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts that chan<sub>g</sub>e them. ADAS (Hu et al., 2025b) searches over a<sub>g</sub>ent desi<sub>g</sub>ns written as code, and ALMA (Xion<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026) searches over memor<sub>y</sub> desi<sub>g</sub>ns written as code and finds desi<sub>g</sub>ns that out<sub>p</sub>erform human-crafted ones in four se<sub>q</sub>uential decision-makin<sub>g</sub> domains. When success and trainin<sub>g</sub> value diver<sub>g</sub>e, choosin<sub>g</sub> what to <sub>p</sub>ractice next (a curriculum) must answer two questions. The first is where another trial is informative, which failure prediction and competence progress answer diferently. The second is what supervision the selected state needs. Gates and safe<sub>g</sub>uards face a <sub>p</sub>ractical dilemma: continuous human monitorin<sub>g</sub> limits scale<sub>,</sub> but removin<sub>g</sub> <sub>supe</sub>r<sub>v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>o</sub>n l<sub>eaves</sub> <sub>u</sub>nf<sub>a</sub>mili<sub>a</sub>r f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>u</sub>r<sub>es</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>a</sub> r<sub>e</sub>li<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> r<sub>espo</sub>n<sub>se.</sub> H<sub>u</sub>m<sub>a</sub>n <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>e</sub>nti<sub>o</sub>n i<sub>s</sub> m<sub>os</sub>t inf<sub>o</sub>rm<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>s e</sub>ith<sub>er a sa</sub>f<sub>e ac</sub>ti<sub>on or</sub> t<sub>rus</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>th<sub>y ev</sub>id<sub>ence:</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore an</sub> i<sub>rrevers</sub>ibl<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>, a</sub>t <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy</sub> h<sub>as</sub> <sub>no</sub>t b<sub>een</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>come</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>s</sub> di<sub>sagree,</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>sa</sub>f<sub>e</sub>t<sub>y-re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t com<sub>p</sub>onent is <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osed for revision. Synthesis

a retry is a further attempt on the same task instance within one episode; a learning round ends with an update from which later episodes start.</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Behavioral improvement</td><td>The revised system executes better under a common task test.</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Bottleneck</td><td>The component hypothesized to limit the next improvement, given the available repairs.</td><td>4.4</td></tr><tr><td>Continued practice</td><td>Whether another informative trial remains safe and affordable.</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Cost accounting</td><td>An account that separates setup and signal construction, acquisition of the revision, downstream training</td><td>4.1</td></tr><tr><td>Evaluator</td><td>A component that supplies outcome judgments for learning: a person, a hand-written check, or a learned reward model such as a success detector.</td><td>2.1</td></tr><tr><td>Improver</td><td>Whatever generates or selects revisions of the learning procedure: a person, a fixed rule, a search process, or a learned component.</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Informative feedback</td><td>Whether experience distinguishes the remaining learning need.</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Later-learning benefit</td><td>An acquired artifact (data, weights, a model,</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Learner</td><td>a skill, or code) helps a subsequent learner.</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Learning procedure</td><td>The decisions that turn experience into updates: what to collect, how to judge it, and how to construct the update.</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Level of support (R1, R2)</td><td>Mechanism only (machinery through which the improver could change), uncontrolled result (a comparison consistent with a link but without a control that withholds only the acquired knowledge), or controlled result (a frozen-knowledge or</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Matched learner</td><td>fixed-improver control with matched learners and budgets). Procedure improvement A revised collector, evaluator, editor, or</td><td>2.2</td></tr><tr><td>Protected success</td><td>updater teaches a matched learner better.
